Tollmien-Schlichting waves near neutral stable curve
Qi Chen, Di Wu, Zhifei Zhang

Abstract
. In this paper, we study the linear stability of boundary layer flows over a flat plate. Tollmien, Schlichting, Lin et al. found that there exists a neutral curve, which consists of two branches: lower branch α l ​ o ​ w ​ ( R ​ e ) \alpha_{low}(Re) and upper branch α u ​ p ​ ( R ​ e ) \alpha_{up}(Re) . Here, α \alpha is the wave number and R ​ e Re is the Reynolds number. For any α ∈ ( α l ​ o ​ w , α u ​ p ) \alpha\in(\alpha_{low},\alpha_{up}) , there exist unstable modes known as Tollmien-Schlichting (T-S) waves to the linearized Navier-Stokes system. These waves play a key role during the early stage of boundary layer transition. In a breakthrough work ( Duke math Jour, 165(2016) ), Grenier, Guo, and Nguyen provided a rigorous construction of the unstable T-S waves. In this paper, we confirm the existence of the neutral stable curve. To achieve this, we develop a more delicate method for solving the Orr-Sommerfeld equation by borrowing some ideas from the triple-deck theory. This approach allows us to construct the T-S waves in a neighborhood of the neutral curve.



1.
Introduction

In this paper, we are concerned with a classical problem in fluid mechanics: the stability and transition of laminar flow. It has been observed in Reynolds’s experiment that the flow is in the laminar state when the Reynolds number (
Re
) is small. When the Reynolds number exceeds a certain critical value, the flow becomes unstable and can transition to turbulence
[
10
]
. To understand this phenomenon, we consider the flow over a flat plate. The motion of the fluid can be described by the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations in a
domain
\Omega=[0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

(1.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}u^{\nu}+u^{\nu}\cdot\nabla u^{\nu}+\nabla p^{\nu}-\nu\Delta u^{\nu}=0\quad\text{in $[0,T]\times\Omega$},\\
&\nabla\cdot u^{\nu}=0\quad\text{in $[0,T]\times\Omega$},\\
&u^{\nu}|_{y=0}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here
u^{\nu}=\big(u^{\nu}_{1},u^{\nu}_{2}\big)
is the velocity field and
p^{\nu}
is the pressure. When
\nu
is very small, a thin boundary layer will be formed in a neighborhood of plate. In this layer, the behavior of the solution could be described by the Prandtl equation:

(1.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &u^{p}\partial_{x}u^{p}+v^{p}\partial_{Y}u^{p}+\partial_{x}p^{e}|_{y=0}=\partial^{2}_{Y}u^{p},\\
&\partial_{x}u^{p}+\partial_{Y}v^{p}=0,\\
&u^{p}|_{Y=0}=v^{p}|_{Y=0}=0,\quad\lim_{Y\rightarrow+\infty}u^{p}(x,Y)=u^{e}_{1}(t,x,0).\end{aligned}\right.

For the outer flow
U^{e}=1,P^{e}=0
, the Prandtl equation (
1.2
) has a self-similar solution called Blasius solution with the form

\displaystyle(u_{B},v_{B})=\Big(f^{\prime}(\zeta),\frac{1}{2\sqrt{x+x_{0}}}(\zeta f^{\prime}(\zeta)-f(\zeta))\Big),

where
\zeta=\frac{Y}{\sqrt{x+x_{0}}}
with
x_{0}>0
as a free parameter, and the profile
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}ff^{\prime\prime}+f^{\prime\prime\prime}=0,\quad f(0)=f^{\prime}(0)=0,\quad\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}f^{\prime}(\zeta)=1.

The Blasius solution has been experimentally confirmed with remarkable accuracy as a basic flow over a flat plate
[
31
]
. In
[
20
]
, Guo and Iyer justified the local Prandtl expansion with the Blasius solution as a leading-order approximation of the steady Navier-Stokes equations, see also
[
11
]
. Iyer and Masmoudi
[
25
]
justified the global Prandtl expansion. In a recent work
[
23
]
, Guo, Wang, and Zhang proved that the Blasius solution is a stable solution of the unsteady Prandtl equation. This provides another indication that the Blasius solution is reasonable as a basic flow. See
[
22
,
20
,
24
,
32
,
33
]
and references therein for more related works.

Considering a local region in the streamwise direction, the flow
u^{\nu}_{s}=\Big(u(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}),0\Big)
with a Blasius profile
u(Y)
can be seen as an approximation of the Blasius solution. To study the instability of the flow at high Reynolds number under the perturbations, we consider the linearized Naiver-Stokes(NS) system around the flow
u_{s}^{\nu}
, which takes as follows,

(1.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v^{\nu}+u(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}})\partial_{x}v^{\nu}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu}}v_{2}^{\nu}(\partial_{Y}u(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}),0)+\nabla q^{\nu}-\nu\Delta v^{\nu}=0,\\
&\nabla\cdot u^{\nu}=0,\\
&v^{\nu}|_{y=0}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

A traditional method studying the linear stability is the spectral analysis, that is, finding the
solution of (
1.3
) in the form
e^{\lambda t}V
. Then
V
solves an eigenvalue problem. We say that the linearized NS system is spectrally unstable if there exists a solution
V
with
\mathrm{Re}\lambda>0
. Otherwise, the linearized NS system is spectrally stable.

To proceed, we reformulate the problem. We first introduce the following rescaled variables:

\displaystyle\tau=\frac{t}{\sqrt{\nu}},\quad X=\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu}},\quad Y=\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}.

Then the linearized NS system (
1.3
) is reduced to

(1.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}v-\sqrt{\nu}\Delta_{X,Y}v+(v_{2}\partial_{Y}u,0)+u\partial_{X}v+\nabla_{X,Y}q=0,\\
&\mathrm{div}_{X,Y}v=0,\quad v|_{Y=0}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

1.1.
Main result

We try to find the solution to the linearized NS system (
1.3
) in the form

(1.5)

\displaystyle v^{\nu}_{TS}=(v_{1}^{\nu},v_{2}^{\nu})=e^{-\mathrm{i}cnt}e^{\mathrm{i}nx}(\partial_{Y}\phi(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}),-\mathrm{i}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}n\phi(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}})),

Let
\alpha:=\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}n
. Then

(1.6)

\displaystyle v_{TS}=(v_{1},v_{2})=e^{-\mathrm{i}\alpha c\tau}e^{\mathrm{i}\alpha X}(\partial_{Y}\phi(Y),-\mathrm{i}\alpha\phi(Y))

is a solution to the rescaled linearized system (
1.4
), where
\phi
is a solution to the Orr-Sommerfeld equation

(1.7)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\phi-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi+u^{\prime\prime}\phi=0,~~\lim_{Y\to\infty}\phi(Y)=0,\\
&\phi(0)=\partial_{Y}\phi(0)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

where
\varepsilon:=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}n}=\frac{\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{i}\alpha}
. We know from (
1.5
) that

•

If
\mathrm{Im}(c)<0
,
|e^{-\mathrm{i}cnt}|=e^{-|\mathrm{Im}(c)|nt}\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, then
v_{TS}^{\nu}
is stable.

•

If
\mathrm{Im}(c)=0
,
|e^{-\mathrm{i}cnt}|\sim 1
as
t\to\infty
, then
v_{TS}^{\nu}
is neutral stable.

•

If
\mathrm{Im}(c)>0
,
|e^{-\mathrm{i}cnt}|=e^{|\mathrm{Im}(c)|nt}\to+\infty
as
t\to\infty
, then
v_{TS}^{\nu}
is unstable.

The unstable (growing) modes are known as Tollmien-Schlichting (T-S) waves, which play a key role during the early stage of boundary layer transition. This instability was first predicted by Tollmien. Later on, Schlichting and Lin carried out a detailed calculation to reproduce Tollmien’s neutral curve via asymptotic analysis
[
26
]
. Roughly speaking, there exist lower and upper marginal stability branches
\alpha_{low}(\nu),\alpha_{up}(\nu)
that depend on the profile
u(Y)
such that when
\alpha\in(\alpha_{low},\alpha_{up})
, the Orr-Sommerfeld equation exist unstable solutions with
\mathrm{Im}(c)>0
. In the breakthrough work
[
15
]
, Grenier, Guo and Nyugen constructed the T-S waves
for
\alpha
lying in an interval
[a,b]
with
\alpha_{low}\ll a<b\ll\alpha_{up}
for generic flows
u(Y)
. Moreover, they developed a robust Rayleigh-Airy iteration method to solve the Orr-Sommerfeld equation. For nonlinear stability results of boundary layer flows, we refer to
[
13
,
14
,
12
,
17
,
18
,
19
,
8
,
9
,
3
,
5
]
.
For further linear stability results concerning channel flow or pipe flow, we refer to
[
30
,
16
,
1
,
2
,
6
,
7
]
.

For subsonic flow, Yang and Zhang
[
34
]
provided the first rigorous construction of compressible Tollmien-Schlichting (T-S) waves of temporal mode by developing a quasi-compressible and Stokes iteration method. Recently, Masmoudi, Wang, and the last two authors constructed spatial and temporal unstable T-S waves for the entire subsonic regime by introducing a new quasi-incompressible-compressible iteration scheme
[
27
]
. They also constructed multiple unstable acoustic modes, known as Mack modes, in the supersonic boundary layer
[
28
]
.

Before stating our main result, we first outline the structural assumptions on the background shear flow:

(1.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&u(0)=0,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}u(Y)=1,\quad u^{\prime}(0)>0,\quad u^{\prime\prime}(0)<0,\\
&\sup_{Y\geq 0}|\partial_{Y}^{k}(u(Y)-1)e^{\eta_{0}Y}|<+\infty,\quad k=0,\dots,4\text{ with }\eta_{0}\geq C>0.\end{split}

For this kind of flows equipped with analyticity, Grenier, Guo and Nyugen
[
15
]
constructed the unstable T-S waves for
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
with
B\ll 1\ll A
, which matches the physical results quite well. However, physical results also suggest that the transition of linear stability occurs on the neutral curve. Recently, Bian and Grenier
[
4
]
made some progress on the existence of the neutral curve for the analytic basic flow.

The main goal of this paper is to confirm the existence of the neutral stable curve. To this end, we will show that the stable and neutrally stable T-S waves actually exist near the neutral
curve for sufficiently small viscosity
\nu
.
Moreover, we will also attempt to elucidate the transition mechanism of linear stability for boundary flows by studying the stability transition of T-S waves.

Our main result is stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
u(Y)
be a shear flow profile satisfying (
1.8
). Then there exist
\nu_{0}\ll 1
and
A\leq 1\leq B
such that for any
\nu\leq\nu_{0}
the following statement holds true. For any
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
, we can find
c(\alpha)
such that there exists a solution
v_{TS}^{\nu}\in W^{2,\infty}
to the linearized Navier-Stokes equation (
1.3
) in the form of

\displaystyle v^{\nu}_{TS}(t,x,y)=e^{-\mathrm{i}c\alpha\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}t}e^{\mathrm{i}\alpha\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}x}(\partial_{Y}\phi(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}),-\mathrm{i}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}n\phi(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}})).

Moreover,

•

There exist
A_{1}\geq A_{c}\geq A_{0}>A
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))<0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))>0.

•

There exsit
B_{0}\leq B_{c}\leq B_{1}<B
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))>0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))<0.

•

If
\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\ll\alpha\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
, then

\displaystyle\alpha\mathrm{Im}(c)\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Let us now provide some remarks regarding our results.

•

In Theorem
1.1
, we prove the existence of the stable Tollmien-Schlichting waves near
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
and
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
. These results suggest that the lower branch of the neutral curve should be located at
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
, and the upper branch of the neutral curve should be located at
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
.

•

We remove the analytic regularity assumption of the profile
u(Y)
in
[
15
]
by introducing a modified Langer transformation.

•

The assumption
u^{\prime\prime}(0)<0
is unnecessary for the construction of T-S wave when
\alpha
is around the lower branch. This allows the Blasius profile. The upper branch depends
on the profile of
u(Y)
, with
\alpha_{up}\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{20}}
for the Blasius profile. In this case, our proof still works for the construction of the unstable T-S waves. However, the proof of the transition for the upper branch would be much involved.

•

Inspired by
[
15
]
, the main strategy for constructing Tollmien-Schlichting waves
v^{\nu}_{TS}
is to use a modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration. This method constructs two linearly independent solutions(slow mode
\phi_{s}
and fast mode
\phi_{f}
) to the homogeneous Orr-Sommerfeld equation without matching the boundary conditions. Subsequently, we obtain the eigenvalue
c
and its properties by solving dispersion equation

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{s}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{s}(0)}=\frac{\phi_{f}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)}.

However, compared with
[
15
]
, the Rayleigh equation with the same eigenvalue
c
is singular and can not approximate the OS equation in the upper and main deck when
\alpha
is on the neutral curve.
One of main improvements and achievements in this paper is that we observe a refined approximation of the OS equation in the upper and main deck when
\alpha
is on the neutral curve.
This ensures the above dispersion relation exists for
c_{i}\leq 0
and leads us to find the transition mechanism of linear stability near the neutral curve.

1.2.
Sketch of the proof

We introduce the following notations of three important operators

(1.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&OS[\cdot]=\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})+u^{\prime\prime},\\
&Ray_{c}[\cdot]=(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})-u^{\prime\prime},\\
&Airy[\cdot]=\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})-(u-c).\end{split}

The key to constructing a non-zero solution to (
1.7
) is to construct two linearly independent solutions
\phi_{s}
and
\phi_{f}
, to the following homogeneous OS equation

\displaystyle OS[\phi]=0,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\phi(Y)=0,

for
\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\lesssim\alpha\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
,
c_{r}\sim\alpha
and
|c_{i}|\ll\alpha
.
Moreover, the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and fast mode
\phi_{f}
are constructed around
\varphi_{Ray}
and
\psi_{a}
respectively, where

\displaystyle Ray_{c}[\varphi_{Ray}]=0,\quad Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{a}]=0,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\varphi_{Ray}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{a}(Y)=0.

1.2.1.
The triple-deck structure and modified Rayleigh equation

In the case where
c_{r}\geq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
, there exist three crucial scales such that the homogeneous OS equation can be approximated by different simpler equations. In details,

\displaystyle OS[\phi]=0\sim\left\{\begin{aligned} &(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi=0,\quad\phi\sim e^{-\alpha Y},\quad Y\sim\alpha^{-1}:\text{ upper deck},\\
&(u-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}\phi-u^{\prime\prime}\phi=0,\quad\phi\sim(u-c)\log(u-c),\quad|Y-Y_{c}|\sim Y_{c}:\text{ main deck,}\\
&Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi]=0,\quad\phi\sim Ai(\kappa(Y-Y_{c})),\quad|Y-Y_{c}|\sim|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}:\text{ lower deck},\end{aligned}\right.

where
u(Y_{c})=c_{r}
,
\kappa=|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})
and
Ai(\cdot)
is the classical Airy function. The lower deck, also known as the sublayer, appears when
Y_{c}\geq|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. From the triple-deck structure, we formally have that

(1.10)

\displaystyle OS[\phi]\sim-Ray_{c}[\phi]\text{ in upper and main deck},

and

\displaystyle OS[\phi]\sim Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi]\text{ in lower deck(sublayer)}.

However, unlike the construction of the unstable mode (
c_{i}>0
) in
[
15
]
, the approximation (
1.10
) fails on the neutral curve. In fact, on the neutral curve
c_{i}=0\Longrightarrow u(Y_{c})-c=0
, the operator
Ray_{c}[\cdot]
is singular.

The main difficulty is finding a good approximation of
OS[\cdot]
in the upper and main deck around the neutral curve.
To address this, our key observation is that

(1.11)

\displaystyle\text{Diffusion effect}\Longrightarrow\text{Regularity}\Longrightarrow\text{Push $c$ to the left in $\mathbb{C}$}.

This means that we can find some
\hat{c}=c+\mathrm{i}c_{0}
with
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi]=(u-\hat{c})(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi-u^{\prime\prime}\varphi

is a good approximation of
OS[\cdot]
for
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq CY_{c}
. Here the size of
c_{0}
is critical because we need to ensure that
\hat{c}
contains a strong enough regularized effect
from the diffusion term(
c_{0}\gg|\varepsilon|
) and that the extra errors
\mathrm{i}c_{0}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi
generated by adding
\mathrm{i}c_{0}
can be treated as a small perturbation. We observe that the decay solution
\varphi_{Ray}
to the homogeneous Rayleigh solution

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}(Y)\sim\underbrace{(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y}}_{\text{upper deck}}+\underbrace{u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha e^{-\eta_{0}Y}}_{\text{main deck}}\text{ for $Y_{c}\lesssim|Y-Y_{c}|$},

which implies that

\displaystyle(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi_{Ray}(Y)\sim\underbrace{u^{\prime\prime}(Y)e^{-\alpha Y}}_{\text{upper deck}}+\underbrace{\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Y)\alpha e^{-\eta_{0}Y}}{u^{\prime}(0)(u-c)}}_{\text{main deck}}\text{ for $Y_{c}\lesssim|Y-Y_{c}|$}.

The influence of the diffusion term for
Y_{c}\lesssim|Y-Y_{c}|
is similar to

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\varphi_{Ray}(Y)\sim\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})(u^{\prime\prime}(Y)e^{-\alpha Y}+\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Y)\alpha e^{-\eta_{0}Y}}{u^{\prime}(0)(u-c)})\sim\underbrace{\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|}_{\text{upper main}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-2}}_{\text{main deck}},

from which, we can guess
c_{0}\in[|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-2},|\varepsilon|]
. On the other hand, it requires
c_{0}\ll|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
to ensure that the extra errors
\mathrm{i}c_{0}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi
can be treated as small perturbation. By the relation
|\varepsilon|=\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-1}
and
\alpha\geq C\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
, we can choose
c_{0}=|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-s}
with
0<s<2
. In our construction, we always take
c_{0}=|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}
. When
\alpha
is on the neutral curve, the approximation of
OS[\cdot]
in the upper and main deck is

(1.12)

\displaystyle-Ray_{\hat{c}}[\cdot]=-(u-\hat{c})(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})+u^{\prime\prime},\quad\hat{c}=c+\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

This is the key observation for constructing slow/fast mode.
The modified Rayleigh operator
Ray_{\hat{c}}[\cdot]
is a perturbation of the singular
Ray_{c}[\cdot]
by incorporating the regularized effect of the diffusion term
\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}
. By (
1.11
) we can say that
Ray_{\hat{c}}[\cdot]
not just approximates the OS operator
OS[\cdot]
at the quantitative level but also at the spectral level.

1.2.2.
The modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration

Inspired by
[
15
]
, we apply a modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration to construct the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and fast mode
\phi_{f}
. Compared with the Rayleigh-Airy iteration introduced in
[
15
]
, we use the modified Rayleigh operator (
1.12
) to approximate the OS operator
OS[\cdot]
, as the original Rayleigh operator
Ray_{c}[\cdot]
is singular and cannot approximate
OS[\cdot]
on the neutral curve.

We introduce the modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration through the following non-homogeneous OS equation:

\displaystyle OS[\phi_{non}]=f,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\phi_{non}(Y)=0.

We define

\displaystyle Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi^{(0)}]=-f,\quad Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi^{(0)}]=-\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\varphi^{(0)}-\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi^{(0)},

and for any
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi^{(j)}]=u^{\prime\prime}\psi^{(j-1)},

\displaystyle Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi^{(j)}]=-\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\varphi^{(j)}-\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi^{(j)}.

Then we can formally obtain that

\displaystyle\phi_{non}(Y)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\varphi^{(k)}+\psi^{(k)}).

Then the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and the fast mode
\phi_{f}
can be constructed in a suitable function space
\mathcal{X}\subset W^{4,\infty}
as follows:

(1.13)

\displaystyle\phi_{s}=\varphi_{Ray,\hat{c}}+\psi_{s}^{(1)}+\tilde{\phi}_{s},\quad\phi_{f}=\psi_{a}+\tilde{\phi}_{f},

where

\displaystyle Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi_{Ray,\hat{c}}]=0,\quad Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{s}^{(1)}]=-\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\varphi_{Ray,\hat{c}}-\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi_{Ray,\hat{c}},

\displaystyle Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{a}]=0

and
\tilde{\phi}_{s}
,
\tilde{\phi}_{f}
are constructed via the modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration by solving the non-homogeneous OS equations

\displaystyle OS[\tilde{\phi}_{s}]=-u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{s}^{(1)},\quad OS[\tilde{\phi}_{f}]=-u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{a}.

Moreover, to ensure the iterative scheme converges, we construct solutions to the non-homogeneous Airy equation with three kinds of source terms matching the triple-deck structure. In detail, we show the estimates for the non-homogeneous Airy equations

\displaystyle Airy[(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{non}]=F\sim\left\{\begin{aligned} &e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad\text{upper deck},\\
&(u-\hat{c})^{-1}e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad\text{main deck},\\
&(u-\hat{c})^{-3}e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad\text{lower deck(sublayer)}.\end{aligned}\right.

1.2.3.
Dispersion relation

With the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and the fast mode
\phi_{f}
in hand, we construct the solution
\phi
to (
1.7
) by taking

\displaystyle\phi=C_{s}\phi_{s}+C_{f}\phi_{f},\text{ with }C_{s}\phi_{s}(0)+C_{f}\phi_{f}(0)=C_{s}\partial_{Y}\phi_{s}(0)+C_{f}\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)=0.

The existence of non-zero
C_{s}
and
C_{f}
is equivalent to the following dispersion relation holding for certain
(\alpha,c)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{s}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{s}(0)}=\frac{\phi_{f}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)},

from which, we infer that

(1.14)

\displaystyle c\sim u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha\underbrace{-\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}}_{\text{related to Airy function}},\quad c_{i}\sim\frac{\alpha^{2}u^{\prime\prime}(0)\pi}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}\underbrace{-\mathrm{Im}\Big(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\Big)}_{\text{related to Airy function}}.

The dispersion relation (
1.14
) holds for the case
-\delta_{1}(\nu)\leq c_{i}
with
\delta_{1}(\nu)>0
. This is guaranteed by constructing the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and the fast mode
\phi_{f}
via the modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration. This allows us to construct the neutral stable mode by solving (
1.14
).
This is our main novelty and improvement compared with
[
15
]

From the above dispersion relation, we can infer that for
\alpha\in(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
with
A_{0}\ll 1\ll B_{0}
,
c_{r}\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
for
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
and
c_{r}\sim\alpha
for
\alpha\gg\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
. Moreover,

•

for
\alpha\in(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
with
B\ll 1

\displaystyle c_{i}\sim-\mathrm{Im}\Big(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\Big)\left\{\begin{aligned} &<0,\quad\alpha=A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\text{ with }A\sim 1,\\
&>0,\quad\alpha\gg\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}.\end{aligned}\right.

•

for
\alpha=B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
with
B\gg 1
,

\displaystyle c_{i}\sim\frac{\alpha^{2}u^{\prime\prime}(0)\pi}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}<0.

Hence, there exist
A_{c}
and
B_{c}
such that
c_{i}=0
for
\alpha=A_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
or
\alpha=B_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
. From the above discussion, we see that the instability originates from the sublayer. However, the stable effects near the lower branch and the upper branch are quite different. Around the lower branch, the transition mechanism is the competition between the stabilizing effect generated by diffusion and the instability caused by the sublayer. Around the upper branch, the stability due to
the structure of the background flow
u^{\prime\prime}(0)<0
suppresses the instability generated by the sublayer when
\alpha
crosses the upper branch of the neutral curve.

1.3.
Organization

The rest of this paper is organized as follows. Section 2 is devoted to the Rayleigh equation, in which we construct the solutions to the Rayleigh equation and show some key estimates. Section 3 contains the constructions of solutions to the Airy equation with three kinds of source terms related to the Rayleigh-Airy iteration. In Section 4, we construct the slow mode and fast mode of the Orr-Sommerfeld equation by a modified Rayleigh-Airy iteration, respectively. In Section 5, we present the proof of Theorem
1.1
by solving the dispersion equation.

2.
The Rayleigh equation

In this section, we consider the non-homogeneous Rayleigh equation on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

(2.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &(u(Y)-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi(Y)-u^{\prime\prime}\varphi(Y)=F(Y),\quad Y>0,\\
&\lim_{Y\to\infty}\varphi(Y)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

and the homogeneous Rayleigh equation:

(2.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &(u(Y)-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi_{Ray}(Y)-u^{\prime\prime}\varphi_{Ray}(Y)=0,\quad Y>0,\\
&\lim_{Y\to\infty}\varphi(Y)=0.\end{aligned}\right.

For convenience, we denote

\displaystyle Ray[\cdot]=(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})-u^{\prime\prime}.

We construct the solution to the Rayleigh equation in the following functional spaces:

(2.3)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\theta}}=

\displaystyle\|(u-c)^{3}\partial_{Y}^{4}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|(u-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{3}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|(u-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|\partial_{Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

and

(2.4)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}=\|(u-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|\partial_{Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

where

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}=\|e^{\theta Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We denote

(2.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{1}=\big\{(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}:(|\alpha|+|c|)|\log c_{i}|\ll 1,c_{r}\geq C_{1}c_{i}>0\big\}.

In this section, we always assume that
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{1}
.

2.1.
Non-homogeneous Rayleigh equation

For the non-homogeneous Rayleigh equation (
2.1
), we have the following result.

Proposition 2.1
.

If
e^{\theta Y}F\in L^{\infty}
, then there exists a solution
\varphi_{non}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}
to (
2.1
) with

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

In addition, if
e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}^{j}F\in L^{\infty}
for
j=1,2
, then

(2.6)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+C\|(u-c)\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+C\|(u-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

Proof.

Applying Proposition 3.2 in
[
27
]
by taking
m=0
, we can obtain

(2.7)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

From (
2.1
) and (
2.7
), we infer that

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(u-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{3}\varphi_{non}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\log c_{i}|\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+C\|(u-c)\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(u-c)^{3}\partial_{Y}^{4}\varphi_{non}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\log c_{i}|\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+C\|(u-c)\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+C\|(u-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

which implies (
2.6
).
∎

2.2.
Homogeneous Rayleigh equation

In this part, we consider the Rayleigh equation on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

(2.8)

\displaystyle(u(Y)-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\varphi(Y)-u^{\prime\prime}(Y)\varphi(Y)=0,\quad Y>0.

Our task is to construct a non-zero decay solution
\varphi_{Ray}(Y)
to (
2.8
). In fact, we construct such solution around
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}
defined by

(2.9)

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}:=2\alpha e^{\alpha Y}(u-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ.

By a direct calculation, we find that

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}]=2\alpha u^{\prime}\varphi^{(0)}_{Ray}.

We define that
\varphi_{R}
is the solution to the equation

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{R}]=-2\alpha u^{\prime}\varphi^{(0)}_{Ray},

which is constructed as in Proposition
2.1
. Then

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}(Y)=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)+\varphi_{R}(Y),

and Proposition
2.1
suggests that
\varphi_{R}(Y)
is a
\mathcal{O}(\alpha)
perturbation. In order to obtain the point-wise estimates and the asymptotic expansion on the boundary for
\varphi_{Ray}
, we first show such estimates for
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}
.

Lemma 2.2
.

There exists
Y_{0}>0
such that

•

For
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{e^{-\alpha Y}(u-c)}{(1-c)^{2}}\right|+\left|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\partial_{Y}\Big(\frac{e^{-\alpha Y}(u-c)}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big)\right|\leq C\alpha e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

•

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{e^{-\alpha Y}(u-c)}{(1-c)^{2}}-\frac{2\alpha e^{-\eta_{0}Y}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\right|\leq C\alpha|u(Y)-c||\log|c_{i}||,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{e^{-\alpha Y}u^{\prime}(Y)}{(1-c)^{2}}\right|\leq C\alpha|\log|c_{i}||.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-c+\frac{2\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|c||\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=u^{\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

Moreover, we have

(2.10)

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-(1-c)^{-2}(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y}\big\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Proof.

According to Lemma 3.3 in
[
27
]
, we can obtain the results in Lemma
2.2
except for (
2.10
). We denote

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}^{(0)}(Y)=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-(1-c)^{-2}(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y}.

Then we have for
j=0,1,2

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{j}Ray_{c}[\tilde{\varphi}^{(0)}]=2\alpha\partial_{Y}^{j}(u^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\varphi}^{(0)}),\quad\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\partial_{Y}\tilde{\varphi}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C\alpha|\log c_{i}|,

which gives (
2.10
).
∎

We provide a more precise expansion of the boundary value of
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}
up to the second order, as the second-order term plays a crucial role in the transition of stability near the upper branch of the neutral curve.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
|c_{i}|\ll\min\{\alpha,c_{r}\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)\right)=-c_{i}-\frac{2\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)\right)=\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha+c_{r}).

Proof.

Estimates of
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)
. By a direct calculation, we can obtain

(2.11)

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-c-2\alpha c\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ+\mathcal{B}_{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}=

\displaystyle c(1-\frac{1}{(1-c)^{2}})+\frac{2\alpha c}{(1-c)^{2}}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}dZ-2\alpha c\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}e^{-2\alpha Z}\left(\frac{1}{(u-c)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)dZ.

We notice that

(2.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{0})|\lesssim&|c|^{2}+\alpha c_{r}+\alpha|c|\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Z}dZ+|c_{i}|c_{r}+\alpha|c|\lesssim\alpha c_{r},\end{split}

and

(2.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{0})|\lesssim&c_{r}|c_{i}|+\alpha|c_{i}|+\alpha|c_{i}|\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Z}dZ+c_{r}|c_{i}|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).\end{split}

Therefore, we need to show an asymptotic formula for
2\alpha c\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ
. In details, we have

(2.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}2\alpha c\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ=-\frac{2\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}+\mathcal{B}_{1},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{1}=-\frac{2\alpha c}{e^{2\alpha}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})(u(Y_{0})-c)}-\frac{4\alpha^{2}c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)}.

Moreover, we notice that

(2.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{1})|\lesssim\alpha c_{r}+\left|\frac{\alpha^{2}c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)}\right|\lesssim\alpha c_{r}+\alpha^{2}c_{r}|\log|c_{i}||\lesssim\alpha c_{r},\end{split}

and

(2.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{1})|\lesssim\alpha|c_{i}|+\left|\frac{\alpha^{2}c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)}\right|\lesssim\alpha c_{i}+\alpha^{2}c_{r}|\log|c_{i}||\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).\end{split}

Therefore, by (
2.11
)-(
2.16
), we obtain

(2.17)

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-c+\frac{2\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}-\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}+\mathcal{B}_{2},

where
\mathcal{B}_{2}=\mathcal{B}_{0}+\mathcal{B}_{1}
with

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{2})|\lesssim\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}),\quad|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{2})|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).

To give more precise expansion of
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)
, we introduce

\displaystyle g(Y)=u^{\prime}(Y)-u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}u^{\prime}(Y)(u(Y)-c_{r}),

which satisfies

\displaystyle g(Y_{c})=0,\quad g^{\prime}(Y_{c})=0\quad\text{and}\quad|g^{\prime\prime}(Y)|\leq C.

Then we have

(2.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}=\frac{2\alpha cu^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\log(-c)+\mathcal{B}_{3},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{3}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha cu^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}e^{-2\alpha Y_{0}}\log(u(Y_{0})-c)-\frac{4\alpha^{2}cu^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}\log(u(Z)-c)dZ

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha cu^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{\mathrm{i}c_{i}u^{\prime}(Z)}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ-\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{g(Z)dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{3,1}+\mathcal{B}_{3,2}+\mathcal{B}_{3,3}+\mathcal{B}_{3,4}.

We also notice that

(2.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{3,1})\right|\lesssim\alpha|c|,\quad\left|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{3,1})\right|\leq C\alpha|c||c_{i}|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}),\\
&\left|\mathcal{B}_{3,2}\right|\leq C\alpha^{2}|c||\log|c_{i}||\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}),\quad\left|\mathcal{B}_{3,3}\right|\leq C\alpha|c_{i}|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).\end{split}

By the definition of
g(Y)
, we find that for any
Y\in[0,Y_{0}]
,
|g(Y)|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2}
, from which we notice that for any
Z\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{g(Z)}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{g(Z)}{(u(Z)-c_{r})^{2}}+g(Z)\left(\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c_{r})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g(Z)}{(u(Z)-c_{r})^{2}}+g(Z)\left(\frac{c_{i}^{2}}{(u-c_{r})^{2}(u-c)^{2}}+\frac{2\mathrm{i}c_{i}}{(u-c_{r})(u-c)^{2}}\right).

Then by the property of
g
, we know that for any
Z\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\left|g(Z)\left(\frac{c_{i}^{2}}{(u-c_{r})^{2}(u-c)^{2}}+\frac{2\mathrm{i}c_{i}}{(u-c_{r})(u-c)^{2}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|c_{i}|\frac{1}{|u(Z)-c|}\leq\frac{C|c_{i}|}{|Z-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|},

which implies that for any
Z\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{g(Z)}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}\right)\right|\leq C+\frac{C|c_{i}|}{|Z-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|},\quad\left|\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{g(Z)}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C|c_{i}|}{|Z-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|}.

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{g(Z)dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}\right)\right|\leq C+C|c_{i}|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{|Z-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|}dZ\leq C(1+|c_{i}||\log|c_{i}||),

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Im}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{g(Z)dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}\right)\right|\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{|Z-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|}dZ\leq C|c_{i}|\log|c_{i}|.

From the above bounds, we get

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{3,4})\right|\leq C\alpha c_{r},\quad\left|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{3,4})\right|\leq C\alpha c_{r}|c_{i}||\log|c_{i}||\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).

which along with (
2.18
) and (
2.19
) imply that

(2.20)

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{3})\right|\leq C\alpha c_{r},\quad\left|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{3})\right|\leq C\alpha|c_{i}|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).

Then by (
2.17
), (
2.18
) and (
2.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-c+\frac{2\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}-\frac{2\alpha cu^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\log(-c)+\mathcal{B}_{4},

where
\mathcal{B}_{4}=\mathcal{B}_{2}+\mathcal{B}_{3}
with

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{B}_{4})\right|\leq C\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}),\quad\left|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{B}_{4})\right|\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).

In particular, we have the following asymptotic expansion for
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)
:

(2.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Im}\left(\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)\right)=-c_{i}-\frac{2\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})).\end{split}

Estimates of
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)
.
We first have

(2.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=&u^{\prime}(0)+2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}(u-c)^{-2}dZ+2\alpha c^{-1}+\mathcal{D}_{0},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{0}=

\displaystyle u^{\prime}(0)((1-c)^{-2}-1)+\alpha\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)-\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)}{(1-c)^{2}}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}dZ

\displaystyle+2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)\int^{Y_{0}}_{0}e^{-2\alpha Z}\Big((u-c)^{-2}-(1-c)^{-2}\Big)dZ.

By a similar argument as in (
2.13
), we have

(2.23)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}\mathcal{D}_{0}|\lesssim\alpha+c_{r},\quad|\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{D}_{0}|\lesssim|c_{i}|+\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})\ll\alpha.

We find that

(2.24)

\displaystyle 2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\alpha Z}\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ+2\alpha c^{-1}=2\alpha\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ+\mathcal{D}_{1},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(0))}{cu^{\prime}(Y_{c})}-\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)}{e^{2\alpha}u(Y_{c})(u(Y_{0})-c)}-\frac{4\alpha^{2}u^{\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{dZ}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)}

\displaystyle+2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}-u^{\prime}(0)^{-1})\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ.

Moreover, we notice that

(2.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{1})|\lesssim\alpha|c_{i}||c|^{-1}+\alpha|c_{i}|+\alpha^{2}|c_{i}|+\alpha c_{r}|\log|c_{i}||\ll\alpha.\end{split}

On the other hand, by a similar argument as in (
2.18
) and (
2.20
), we obtain

(2.26)

\displaystyle 2\alpha\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Z))}{e^{2\alpha Z}(u(Z)-c)^{2}}dZ=\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\log(-c)+\mathcal{D}_{2},

where

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{D}_{2})|\lesssim\alpha,\quad|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{2})|\lesssim\alpha c_{r}|\log|c_{i}||+|c_{i}|\ll\alpha.

Therefore, by (
2.22
)-(
2.26
), we obtain

(2.27)

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=

\displaystyle u^{\prime}(0)+\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\log(-c)+\mathcal{D}_{3},

where
\mathcal{D}_{3}=\mathcal{D}_{0}+\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{D}_{3})|\lesssim\alpha,\quad|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{3})|\lesssim\alpha c_{r}|\log|c_{i}||+|c_{i}|\ll\alpha.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\left(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)\right)=u^{\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}(\alpha),\quad\mathrm{Im}\left(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)\right)=\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha).

The proof is completed. ∎

Now we are in a position to construct the solution
\varphi_{Ray}
to the homogeneous Rayleigh equation. Recall that

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}]=2\alpha u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.

As we mentioned at the beginning of this subsection, we shall construct
\varphi_{Ray}
in the form of

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}(Y)=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)+\varphi_{R}(Y),

where
\varphi_{R}=\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}
with

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-2\alpha(u-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(u-c)^{-2}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime},

and
Ray[\varphi_{2}]=\alpha^{2}(u-c)\varphi_{1}
constructed in Proposition
2.1
.

Lemma 2.4
.

It holds that

•

For
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

•

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}(Y)=-\frac{\alpha e^{-\alpha Y}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|u-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)=\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}(0)=-\frac{\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|^{2}+|c|^{2})|\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(0)=\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

Moreover, we have

(2.28)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{R}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Proof.

According to Lemma 3.4 in
[
27
]
, we can obtain the results in the above lemma except for (
2.28
). Moreover, from Lemma 3.4 in
[
27
]
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{R}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C\alpha|\log c_{i}|,

which along with (
2.10
) and the fact
\partial_{Y}^{j}Ray_{c}[\varphi_{R}]=-2\alpha\partial_{Y}(u^{\prime}\varphi^{(0)}_{Ray})
gives (
2.28
).
∎

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
|c_{i}|\ll\min\{\alpha,c_{r}\}
. We obtain the following estimates for
\varphi_{R}(0)
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\varphi_{R}(0)\right)=\frac{\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})),

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(0)\right)=\frac{\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha+c_{r}),

Proof.

According to the definition of
\varphi_{R}
, we know that
\varphi_{R}(0)=\varphi_{1}(0)+\varphi_{2}(0)
. Moreover, by Proposition
2.1
and Lemma
2.4
,
we can obtain

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{2}(0)|+|\varphi_{2}(0)|\lesssim\alpha^{2}(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||\ll\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).

Then we have

(2.29)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{R}(0))=\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{1}(0))+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})),\\
&\mathrm{Im}(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(0))=\mathrm{Im}(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(0))+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})).\end{split}

Hence, we are left with the estimates for
\varphi_{1}(0)
and
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(0)
. We first notice that

(2.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi_{1}(0)=&2\alpha c\int_{0}^{+\infty}(u-c)^{-2}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.\end{split}

where

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})}{(u(Y^{\prime})-c)^{2}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY,

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}(Y)dY-\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}u^{\prime}(u-c)^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY,

\displaystyle I_{3}=

\displaystyle 2\alpha c\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}(u-c)^{-2}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY-\frac{2\alpha c}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})(u(Y_{0})-c))}\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY.

By Lemma
2.2
, we know that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(2.31)

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)=(u-c)(1-c)^{-1}e^{-\alpha Y}+\mathcal{E}(Y),\quad|\mathcal{E}(Y)|\lesssim(\alpha+c_{r})e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

Moreover, we have that for
Y\geq Y_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{E}(Y))=

\displaystyle 2\alpha e^{\alpha Y}\mathrm{Im}\left((u-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-2\alpha Z}\left(\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)dZ\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\alpha c_{i}e^{-\alpha Y}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-2\alpha Z}\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)dZ

\displaystyle+2\alpha(u-c_{r})e^{-\alpha Y}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-2\alpha Z}\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{1}{(u(Z)-c)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)dZ,

which implies that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{E}(Y))|\lesssim\alpha|c_{i}|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad\forall Y\geq Y_{0}.

This along with the fact
|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{E}(Y))|\lesssim\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||
for
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
in Lemma
2.2
deduces that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(2.32)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{E}(Y))|\lesssim\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

Therefore, we notice that for any
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(2.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\mathrm{Im}\Big(\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}\Big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\Big|\mathrm{Im}\Big((1-c)^{-1}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}\Big)\Big|+\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}|\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{E}(Y^{\prime}))|dY^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||.\end{split}

Moreover, for any
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(2.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}\\
&=(1-c)^{-1}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}+\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\mathcal{E}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{Y}^{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u(Y^{\prime})-c)dY^{\prime}+\mathcal{I},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}=

\displaystyle(1-c)^{-1}\int_{Y}^{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}-\int_{Y}^{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u(Y^{\prime})-c)dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+(1-c)^{-1}\int_{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}+\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\mathcal{E}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}.

By integration by parts, we have that for
Y\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})(u(Y^{\prime})-c)dY^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}(u(\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}})-c)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(u(Y)-c)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}(u(Y)-c)^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha+c_{r}).

About
\mathcal{I}
, we have that for
Y\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}(Y)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|1-(1-c)^{-1}|\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}+\int_{Y}^{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\eta_{0}Y}|e^{\alpha Y^{\prime}}-1|dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}+\alpha\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}\lesssim\alpha+c_{r}.

From (
2.34
) and the above two estimates, we can deduce that for
Y\in[0,Y_{0}]
,

(2.35)

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}(u(Y)-c)^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha+c_{r}).

By a similar argument as in (
2.18
)-(
2.20
) and applying the estimates (
2.33
) and (
2.35
), we obtain

(2.36)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(I_{1})=\frac{\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})).

About
I_{2}
, we notice that by (
2.33
),

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Im}(I_{2})|\lesssim\alpha^{2}(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||+\alpha c_{r}\Big|\mathrm{Im}\Big(\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}u^{\prime}(u-c)^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY\Big)\Big|.

On the other hand, by (
2.31
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathrm{Im}\Big(\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}u^{\prime}(u-c)^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY\Big)\Big|\lesssim c_{i}+\Big|\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}u^{\prime}(u-c)^{-1}\mathcal{E}(Y)dY\Big|\lesssim(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||.

Therefore, we obtain

(2.37)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Im}(I_{2})|\lesssim\alpha^{2}(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||.

Again by (
2.31
) and (
2.33
), we can obtain

(2.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathrm{Im}(I_{3})|\lesssim&\alpha c_{r}|c_{i}|\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}|u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})|dY^{\prime}+\alpha^{2}c_{r}\int_{Y_{0}}^{\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Y}dY+\alpha^{2}(\alpha+c_{r})\\
\ll&\alpha(\alpha+c_{r}).\end{split}

Therefore, by (
2.29
), (
2.30
), (
2.36
)-(
2.38
), we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\varphi_{R}(0)\right)=\frac{\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})).

Now we are in a position to show the estimates for
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(0)
. We first notice that

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(0)=

\displaystyle-2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)\int_{0}^{+\infty}(u-c)^{-2}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY

\displaystyle+2\alpha c^{-1}\int_{0}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY=II_{1}+II_{2}+II_{3},

where

\displaystyle II_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{u^{\prime}(Y)-u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}{(u-c)^{2}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY,

\displaystyle II_{1}=

\displaystyle 2\alpha c^{-1}\Big(1-\frac{u^{\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\Big)\int_{0}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}(Y)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY-\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{0}^{Y_{0}}\frac{u^{\prime}}{u-c}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY,

and

\displaystyle II_{3}=

\displaystyle-2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}(u-c)^{-2}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y^{\prime})dY^{\prime}dY

\displaystyle+\frac{2\alpha u^{\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})(u(Y_{0})-c)}\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}u^{\prime}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)dY.

By a similar argument in the estimates of
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{R}(0))
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(II_{1})=\frac{\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha+c_{r}),\quad|\mathrm{Im}(II_{2})|+|\mathrm{Im}(II_{3})|\ll\alpha+c_{r}.

The proof is completed.
∎

From Lemma
2.2
-
2.5
, we directly conclude that

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{1}
. There exists a solution
\varphi_{Ray}\in\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}
to (
2.8
), for which it holds that

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}(0)=-c+\frac{\alpha}{u(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}((|\alpha|^{2}+|c|^{2})|\log|c_{i}||),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}(0)=u^{\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log|c_{i}||).

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{Ray}-(u-c)e^{-\alpha Y}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

In particular, for
|c_{i}|\ll\min\{\alpha,c_{r}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\varphi_{Ray}(0)\right)=-c_{i}-\frac{\alpha c_{r}u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{3}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha(\alpha+c_{r})),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}(0)\right)=\frac{\alpha u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{2}}\arg(-c)+o(\alpha+c_{r}).

3.
The Airy equation

In this section, we consider the following Airy equation

(3.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\psi-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi=F,\quad Y>0,\\
&\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi(Y)=0,\end{split}

with several kinds of source terms
F
. We construct a solution
\psi_{a}
to the corresponding homogeneous Airy equation around a modified Airy function

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\psi_{a}-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{a}=0,\quad Y>0,

\displaystyle\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{a}(Y)=0.

We define

\displaystyle\eta_{out}(Y):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\frac{3}{2}\int_{Y_{c}}^{Y}\left(\frac{u(Z)-c_{r}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dZ\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad Y\geq Y_{c},\\
-\left(\frac{3}{2}\int^{Y_{c}}_{Y}\left(\frac{c_{r}-u(Z)}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dZ\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad Y\leq Y_{c},\end{array}\right.

and
\eta_{in}(Y):=Y-Y_{c}
. Then we introduce the modified Langer transformation

(3.2)

\displaystyle\eta(Y):=\eta_{r}(Y)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{i}

with
\eta_{i}:=-u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}(|\varepsilon|\alpha^{2}+c_{i})
and

(3.3)

\displaystyle\eta_{r}(Y):=\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\eta_{in}(Y)+(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big))\eta_{out}(Y),

where
\chi(\cdot)
is a smooth function in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
\chi(Y)\equiv 1
on
[0,1]
and
\chi(Y)=0
on
[2,+\infty)
. We also introduce the modified Airy function

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y):=-\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)),~~A_{2}(Y):=2\pi Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))

with
\kappa:=|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{\frac{1}{3}}
matching the scale of sublayer. We also define

\displaystyle A_{1}(1,Y):=-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(Z)dZ,\qquad A_{1}(2,Y)=-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(1,Z)dZ,

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y):=\int_{-\infty}^{Y}A_{2}(Z)dZ,\qquad A_{2}(2,Y)=\int_{-\infty}^{Y}A_{2}(1,Z)dZ.

By the properties of Airy function and the above definitions, we can obtain by direct calculations that for
j=1,2
,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})A_{j}(Y)-(u-c)A_{j}(Y)=Err_{1}(Y)A_{j}+Err_{2}(Y)\partial_{Y}A_{j},

where

\displaystyle Err_{1}(Y):=u^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta(\partial_{Y}\eta)^{2}-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}-(u-c)\text{ and }Err_{2}(Y):=\frac{\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta(Y)}{\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)}.

We shall see that
Err_{1}
and
Err_{2}
are, in fact, small errors.
Moreover, by the property of Airy function, we obtain

(3.5)

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y)\partial_{Y}A_{2}(Y)-(\partial_{Y}A_{1}(Y))A_{2}(Y)=-\varepsilon^{-1}\partial_{Y}\eta(Y).

Hence, for given source term
e^{\theta Y}F\in L^{\infty}
, we define

(3.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}w_{app}(Y):=&A_{1}(Y)\int_{0}^{Y}A_{2}(Z)\partial_{Y}\eta(Z)^{-1}F(Z)dZ\\
&+A_{2}(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(Z)\partial_{Y}\eta(Z)^{-1}F(Z)dZ,\\
\psi_{app}(Y):=&\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}w_{app}(Z)dZ.\end{split}

By direct calculations, we know that
\psi_{app}
is a solution to the following equation

(3.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})w_{app}-(u-c)w_{app}=F+Err_{1}w_{app}+Err_{2}\partial_{Y}w_{app},\\
&\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi_{app}(Y)=w_{app}(Y),\quad\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{app}(Y)=w_{app}(Y)=0.\end{split}

We will show that (
3.7
) is an appropriate approximation of (
3.1
).

In the rest of this section, we decompose
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{0\}=\mathcal{N}^{-}\cup\mathcal{N}\cup\mathcal{N}^{+},
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{+}:=\{Y\geq Y_{c}:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M\},\quad\mathcal{N}:=\{Y:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\leq M\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{-}:=\{0\leq Y\leq Y_{c}:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M\}.

We always assume that the structure assumption (
1.8
) holds and
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}
, where

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{2}:=\{(\alpha,c):|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq C\min(\alpha,c_{r}),(\alpha+c_{r})|\log|c_{i}||+(\alpha^{2}+c_{r}^{2})|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\ll 1,|c_{i}|\ll\min\{\alpha,c_{r}\}\}.

It is easy to check that for
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
, we always have
-\delta_{0}<\mathrm{Im}(\kappa\eta(Y))<\delta_{0}
, where
\delta_{0}
is a small constant as defined in Lemma
A.1
.

3.1.
Estimates of the Airy function

Since we introduce the modified Langer transformation involved in the construction of
w_{app}
, we first show some useful estimates for
\eta(Y)
and the modified Airy function.
The proof of the following results is presented in
[
27
]
.

Lemma 3.1

(Lemma 4.1 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
\alpha,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
and
c_{r}>0
. Suppose that
u(Y)
satisfies the structure assumption (
1.8
) and
0<L\leq 1
is a small number. Then
\eta_{r}(Y)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
\eta_{r}(Y)
satisfies that following properties:

(1)

For any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle|\eta_{r}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2},

and for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
,

\displaystyle C^{-1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq|\eta_{r}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

(2)

There exists
0<C_{1}\leq C_{2}<+\infty
such that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle C_{1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}\leq|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}(Y)|\leq C_{2}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Moreover, for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}(Y)-1|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|.

(3)

For any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

Here the constant
C
is independent of
\eta_{0}
.

Lemma 3.2

(Lemma 4.3 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
\alpha,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
and
c_{r}>0
,
|c_{i}|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. Suppose that
u(Y)
satisfies the structure assumption (
1.8
) and
0<L\leq 1
is a small number. Then

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|\eta_{r}(Y)|(|c_{i}|+|\eta_{r}(Y)|),\qquad\forall|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}||\eta_{r}(Y)|,\qquad 2\kappa^{-1}M\leq|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}|,\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L,

and

\displaystyle|Err_{2}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|.

Now we show the asymptotic expansion of the primitives of the modified Airy function.

Lemma 3.3

(Lemma A.4 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
\alpha,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
and
c_{r}>0
. Suppose that
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1\leq M
are the constants in Lemma
A.1
. Then there holds that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}\cup\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

\displaystyle A_{1}(1,Y)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\partial_{Y}\eta^{-1}\Big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\Big)^{-\frac{3}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}\big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{O}(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}})),

\displaystyle A_{1}(2,Y)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\partial_{Y}\eta^{-2}\Big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\Big)^{-\frac{5}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}\big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{O}(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}})),

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\pi}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\partial_{Y}\eta^{-1}\Big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\Big)^{-\frac{3}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}\big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{O}(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}})),

\displaystyle A_{2}(2,Y)=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\pi}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\partial_{Y}\eta^{-2}\Big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\Big)^{-\frac{5}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}\big(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{O}(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}})),

and for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}
,

\displaystyle|A_{1}(1,Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}},\quad|A_{1}(2,Y)|\leq C,

\displaystyle|A_{2}(1,Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}},\quad|A_{2}(2,Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}.

At the end of this part, we show the estimates for the Green function of the approximate Airy equation (
3.7
).

Lemma 3.4

(Lemma 4.4 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1\leq M
be the constants as defined in Lemma
A.1
. Suppose
|\kappa\eta_{i}|<\delta_{0}
and
\alpha,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
. Then for any
0\leq Z\leq Y
, there exists
\gamma_{0}>0
such that for
k=0,1,2
and
j=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2+k-j}{3}}\mathcal{M}(k,Y)\mathcal{M}(-j,Z)e^{-W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)},

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2+k-j}{3}}\mathcal{M}(k,Z)\mathcal{M}(-j,Y)e^{-W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)},

where
\mathcal{M}(l;Y)=|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{l}|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{l}{2}}
for
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}\cup\mathcal{N}^{+}
,
\mathcal{M}(l;Y)=M^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{l}{2}}
for
Y\in\mathcal{N}
and

(3.8)

\displaystyle W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)=\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Y)-\eta(Z)|(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

3.2.
Approximate solution to Airy equation with good force

In this part, we show estimates about
\psi_{app}
and
w_{app}
defined in (
3.6
) satisfying the approximate Airy equation (
3.7
) for the source term
F
corresponding to the upper and main deck. In a similar way as in Proposition 4.5 in
[
27
]
, we can have the following results.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
1\lesssim\theta\leq 3\eta_{0}
and
\hat{c}:=c+\mathrm{i}c_{0}
with
c_{0}\in(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}},|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}})
. Suppose
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
with
c_{i}>-c_{0}/2
and
e^{\theta Y}F\in L^{\infty}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-\hat{c})w_{app}(Y)|+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}w_{app}(Y)|+|\varepsilon||e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}^{2}w_{app}(Y)|\leq C\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}\psi_{app}(Y)|+|e^{\theta Y}\psi_{app}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}Err_{1}(Y)w_{app}(Y)|+|e^{\theta Y}Err_{2}(Y)\partial_{Y}w_{app}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

Now we show the estimates about
w_{app}
with the source term
F\sim(u-\hat{c})^{-1}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
1\lesssim\theta\leq 3\eta_{0}
and
\hat{c}:=c+\mathrm{i}c_{0}
with
c_{0}\in(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}},|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}})
. Suppose
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
with
c_{i}>-c_{0}/2
and
e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-\hat{c})F\in L^{\infty}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle|(u-\hat{c})^{2}w_{app}(Y)|+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}w_{app}(Y)|+|\varepsilon||(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}^{2}w_{app}(Y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|\log c_{i}|e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{app}(Y)|+|e^{\theta Y}\psi_{app}(Y)|\leq C(1+|c||\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}})|\log c_{i}|^{2}\|(u-\hat{c})F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle|(u-\hat{c})Err_{1}(Y)w_{app}(Y)|+|(u-\hat{c})Err_{2}(Y)\partial_{Y}w_{app}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}|\log c_{i}|e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})F\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}.

3.3.
Approximate solution to the Airy equation with singular force

In this part, we construct a solution to

(3.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})w_{app,s}-(u-c)w_{app,s}=\partial_{Y}^{2}G(Y)+Err_{1}w_{app,s}+Err_{2}\partial_{Y}w_{app,s},\\
&\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi_{app,s}(Y)=w_{app,s}(Y),\quad\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{app,s}(Y)=w_{app,s}(Y)=0,\end{split}

for some
G(Y)
containing the singularity around the critical layer. By integration by parts, the solution
\psi_{app}(Y)
to (
3.9
) can be written as

(3.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{app,s}(Y)=\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{j}(Y)+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}(Y),\end{split}

where

(3.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y):=&-A_{1}(2,Y)A_{2}(0)\partial_{Y}\eta(0)^{-1}\partial_{Y}G(0)+A_{1}(2,Y)\partial_{Y}A_{2}(0)\partial_{Y}\eta(0)^{-1}G(0),\\
\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y):=&A_{1}(2,Y)\int_{0}^{Y}\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{2}(\partial_{Z}\eta)^{-1}G(Z)dZ+A_{2}(2,Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{1}(\partial_{Z}\eta)^{-1}G(Z)dZ,\\
\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y):=&\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(Z-Y)\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ,\\
\mathcal{M}_{4}(Y):=&\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\mathcal{(}A_{1}(2,Z)\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{2}(Z)-A_{2}(2,Z)\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{1}(Z))\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ,\\
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(0)}(Y):=&A_{1}(2,Y)\int_{0}^{Y}\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}\Big(A_{2}(Z)\partial_{Z}G(Z)-\partial_{Z}A_{2}(Z)G(Z)\Big)dZ\\
&+A_{2}(2,Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}\Big(A_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}G(Z)-\partial_{Z}A_{1}(Z)G(Z)\Big)dZ,\\
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(0)}(Y):=&\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(Z-Y)\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{2}(Z)dZ,\quad\mathcal{E}_{3}^{(0)}(Y):=\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{3}(Z)dZ\end{split}

with

(3.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z):=&\partial_{Z}A_{1}(Z)A_{2}(Z)-\partial_{Z}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(Z)+A_{2}(1,Z)\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{1}(Z)-A_{1}(1,Z)\partial_{Z}^{2}A_{2}(Z),\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}(Z):=&(A_{2}(1,Z)A_{1}(Z)-A_{1}(1,Z)A_{2}(Z))\partial_{Z}G(Z)\\
&-(A_{2}(1,Z)\partial_{Z}A_{1}(Z)-A_{1}(1,Z)\partial_{Z}A_{2}(Z))G(Z),\\
\mathcal{H}_{3}(Z):=&(A_{1}(2,Z)A_{2}(Z)-A_{2}(2,Z)A_{1}(Z))\partial_{Z}G(Z)\\
&-(A_{1}(2,Z)\partial_{Y}A_{2}(Z)-A_{2}(2,Z)\partial_{Z}A_{1}(Z))G(Z).\end{split}

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
1\lesssim\theta\leq 3\eta_{0}
and
\hat{c}:=c+\mathrm{i}c_{0}
with
c_{0}\in(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}},|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}})
. Suppose
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
with
c_{i}>-c_{0}/2
. Then for any
G(Y)\in W^{2,\infty}
with
\mathrm{supp}(G)\subset[0,\tilde{Y}]
,
\tilde{Y}\sim 1
,
\psi_{app,s}(Y)
defined by (
3.10
) is a solution to (
3.9
) and satisfies that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\psi_{app,s}(Y)|+|e^{\theta Y}(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{app,s}|

\displaystyle\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-1}|\hat{c}||\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi_{app,s}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||(\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}),

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}w_{app,s}(Y)|\leq C(|\varepsilon|^{-1}|\hat{c}_{i}|^{-1}+|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{4}{3}})|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Moreover, for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}\cup\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\psi_{app,s}(Y)|+|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{app,s}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||(\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}).

For any
Y\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y):=\{Z\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cup\mathcal{N}^{-}:|\kappa(\eta(Z)-\eta(Y))|\leq 1\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{far}(Y):=\{Z\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cup\mathcal{N}^{-}:|\kappa(\eta(Z)-\eta(Y))|>1\}.

For
Z,Y\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle e^{\theta|Y-Z|}\leq Ce^{C|\eta_{r}(Y)^{\frac{3}{2}}-\eta_{r}(Z)^{\frac{3}{2}}|}\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{C|\eta_{r}(Y)-\eta_{r}(Z)|(|\eta_{r}(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\eta_{r}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Y)-\eta(Z)|(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})},

which implies that

(3.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)e^{\theta|Y-Z|}\leq&e^{-C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Y)-\eta(Z)|(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})(1+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}})}\\
\leq&e^{-\tilde{C}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Y)-\eta(Z)|(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})}:=\tilde{W}_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z).\end{split}

By the definition of
\mathcal{N}^{-},\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}^{+}
, there exist
Y_{-}
and
Y_{+}
such that

(3.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{-}=[0,Y_{-}],\quad\mathcal{N}=[Y_{-},Y_{+}]\text{ and }\mathcal{N}^{+}=[Y_{+},+\infty).

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Estimates about
\psi_{app,s}(Y)
.

Estimates about
\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y)
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y)
, we first notice that by Lemma
3.1
and
3.4
, for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

(3.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y)|\leq&Ce^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}Y}|\partial_{Y}\eta(0)|^{-1}|\partial_{Y}G(0)|+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}Y}|\partial_{Y}\eta(0)|^{-1}|G(0)|\\
\leq&C|\hat{c}|^{-2}e^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}Y}|\partial_{Y}\eta(0)|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\hat{c}|^{-1}e^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}Y}|\partial_{Y}\eta(0)|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C|\hat{c}|^{-2}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\hat{c}|^{-1}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

Again by Lemma
3.1
and
3.4
, for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}\cup\mathcal{N}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\partial_{Y}G(0)|+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|G(0)|\lesssim|\hat{c}|^{-2}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\hat{c}|^{-1}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}},

which along with (
3.15
) implies that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{M}_{1}(Y)|\leq&C|\hat{c}|^{-2}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\hat{c}|^{-1}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}(\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}).\end{split}

Estimates about
\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)
.
We notice that for any
Y\in\{Y:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1\}
,

(3.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)\leq&C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y)}\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}}e^{-W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}|G(Z)|dZ\\
&+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{far}(Y)}\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}}e^{-W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}|G(Z)|dZ\\
&+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{N}}|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{-1}e^{-W_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}|G(Z)|dZ=\mathcal{I}_{2,1}+\mathcal{I}_{2,2}+\mathcal{I}_{3,3}.\end{split}

By Lemma
3.1
, for any
Y\in\{Y\geq 0:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1,|Y-Y|\leq L\}
and
Z\in\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}|u(Z)-c|}\leq C|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\eta(Z)|^{-1}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}},

and for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
and
Z\in\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y)
,

\displaystyle\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}|u(Z)-c|}\leq C|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{-1}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{6}}.

Therefore, we obtain that for any
Y\in\{Y:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1\}
,

\displaystyle|\kappa\eta(Y)

\displaystyle|^{-\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{-2}|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)||u(Z)-\hat{c})|^{-1}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Then by the above estimates and (
3.13
), we obtain that for any
Y\in\{Y:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1\}
,

(3.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}\lesssim&|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|e^{\theta_{0}Y}(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y)}\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|}e^{-\tilde{W}_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}dZ\\
\lesssim&|\varepsilon|^{-1}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{near}(Y)}1dZ\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

By Lemma
3.1
, for any
Y\in\{Y\geq 0:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1\}
and
Z\in\mathcal{N}_{far}(Y)
, we have

\displaystyle|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|e^{-\tilde{W}_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}\leq C|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}e^{-|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq Ce^{-C|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Therefore, for
\mathcal{I}_{2,2}
, we have

(3.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{2,2}&\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|e^{\theta_{0}Y}(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{far}(Y)}\frac{|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|}{|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)|^{2}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|}e^{-\tilde{W}_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}dZ\\
&\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{-1}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{far}(Y)}e^{-|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|dZ\\
&\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

For
\mathcal{I}_{2,3}
, we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2,3}\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Z\in\mathcal{N}}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||,

which along with (
3.17
), (
3.18
) and (
3.19
) deduces that for any
Y\in\{Y:|\kappa\eta(Y)|\geq M+1\}
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)|\leq Ce^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||.

Now we consider the estimates of
\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)
for the case of
|\kappa\eta(Y)|\leq M+1
. By Lemma
3.4
, we have that for any
|\kappa\eta(Y)|\leq M+1
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{|\kappa\eta(Z)|\geq M+2}|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)|e^{-\tilde{W}_{\varepsilon}(Y,Z)}|G|dZ+|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{|\kappa\eta(Z)|\leq M+2}|G(Z)|dZ

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{|\kappa\eta(Z)|\geq M+2}|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-C|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}dZ

\displaystyle+|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{|\kappa\eta(Z)|\leq M+2}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||.

Therefore, we obtain that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(3.20)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{2}(Y)|\leq Ce^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||.

Estimates about
\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)
.
Notice that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)=\partial_{Z}\Big(\underbrace{\partial_{Z}A_{1}(Z)A_{2}(1,Z)-A_{1}(1,Z)\partial_{Z}A_{2}(Z)}_{\tilde{\mathcal{H}}(Z)}\Big).

Hence, we have that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)&=-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(Z-Y)\tilde{\mathcal{H}}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}\partial_{Z}G(Z)dZ-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ\\
&\quad+\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(Z-Y)\tilde{\mathcal{H}}(Z)\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}G(Z)dZ=\mathcal{I}_{3,1}+\mathcal{I}_{3,2}+\mathcal{I}_{3,3}.\end{split}

We first notice that by Lemma
3.4
, for any
Y\geq Y_{c}+L
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{3,1}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}(Z-Y)|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{Z}G(Z)|dZ\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Again by Lemma
3.4
, for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cap\{Y:|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L\}
,

(3.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{I}_{3,1}(Y)|\leq&|\mathcal{I}_{3,1}(Y_{c}+L)|+\Big|(Y_{c}+L-Y)\int_{Y_{c}+L}^{+\infty}|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{Z}G|dZ\Big|\\
&+\Big|\int_{Y}^{Y_{c}+L}(Z-Y)\tilde{\mathcal{H}}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}\partial_{Z}G(Z)dZ\Big|\\
\leq&C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{c}+L}|Z-Y||\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{Z}G(Z)|dZ.\end{split}

Moreover, for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cap\{Y:|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{Y_{c}+L}

\displaystyle|Z-Y||\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{Z}G(Z)|dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{c}+L}|Z-Y_{c}||\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-2}dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{c}+L}|\kappa\eta(Z)|^{-\frac{3}{2}}dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}},

which along with (
3.22
) implies that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

(3.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big|\mathcal{I}_{3,1}(Y)\Big|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

By a similar argument as above, we can also deduce that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

(3.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big|\mathcal{I}_{3,2}(Y)\Big|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

About
\mathcal{I}_{3,3}
, we get by Lemma
3.1
that

(3.25)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta(Z)}{\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{2}}\right|\leq C.

Hence, again by performing a similar argument in the estimates for
\mathcal{I}_{3,1}
, we have that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{I}_{3,3}(Y)\Big|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}(|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||+|c||\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}})e^{-\theta Y}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}},

which along with (
3.21
), (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) implies that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}
,

(3.26)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||e^{-\theta Y}(\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}).

For any
Y\in\mathcal{N}
, we can write that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y_{+})+(Y_{+}-Y)\int_{Y_{+}}^{+\infty}\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ

\displaystyle+\int_{Y}^{Y_{+}}(Z-Y)\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ=\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y_{+})+\mathcal{I}_{3,4}^{+}+\mathcal{I}_{3,5}^{+}.

By the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and Lemma
3.4
, we infer that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{3,4}^{+}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y_{+}}^{1}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||,

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{3,5}^{+}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{+}}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||.

Therefore, we obtain that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}
,

(3.27)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||(\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}).

For any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}
, notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(Y_{c}-Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ+\int_{Y}^{Y_{-}}(Z-Y_{c})\mathcal{H}_{1}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}G(Z)dZ}_{\mathcal{I}_{3,4}^{-}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{(Y_{-}-Y_{c})\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\mathcal{H}_{1}\partial_{Z}\eta^{-1}G(Z)dZ}_{\mathcal{I}_{3,5}^{-}}+\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y_{-}).

By the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and Lemma
3.4
, we infer that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{3,4}^{-}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|Y-Y_{c}||\varepsilon|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{-}}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle+C|\varepsilon|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{-}}1dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|Y-Y_{c}||\varepsilon|^{-1}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log\hat{c}_{i}|,

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{3,5}^{-}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y_{-}}^{1}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Therefore, we conclude that for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{-}
,

(3.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)|&\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-1}|Y-Y_{c}||\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

By (
3.26
), (
3.27
) and (
3.28
), we infer that for any
Y\geq 0
,

(3.29)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|e^{\theta Y}\mathcal{M}_{3}(Y)|\leq&C|\varepsilon|^{-1}|\log\hat{c}_{i}|(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+(Y-Y_{c})\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}^{-}})\|(u-\hat{c})G\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\log\hat{c}_{i}|\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

Estimates about
\mathcal{M}_{4}(Y)
.
We can obtain the following bounds for
\mathcal{M}_{4}(Y)
by a similar argument as above

(3.30)
