\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle=u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ+\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}[u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)]dZ.

On the other hand, by lemma
3.1
and
3.4
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}[u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)]dZ\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{r}\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}|A_{2}(1,Z)A_{1}(Z)-A_{1}(1,Z)A_{2}(Z)|\leq Cc_{r}.

Hence, we obtain

(4.32)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}dZ=u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ+\mathcal{O}(c_{r}).

By Lemma
A.4
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ=

\displaystyle\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}Hi(e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\kappa\eta(Z))\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ+\mathcal{O}(c_{r}^{2}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\pi u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}\int_{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\kappa\eta(0)}^{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\kappa\eta(2Y_{c})}Hi(-z)dz+\mathcal{O}(c_{r}^{2}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}).

Hence, by Lemma
A.3
and applying the optimal truncation of the asymptotic expansion (
A.2
), we can obtain

(4.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ\Big|\leq C,\\
&\mathrm{Im}\Big(\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)dZ\Big)=\frac{-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\pi}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|c_{r}^{-3}+c^{2}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}).\end{split}

Therefore, by (
4.32
) and (
4.33
), we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}dZ\Big|\leq C,\\
&\mathrm{Im}\Big(\int_{0}^{2Y_{c}}\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\partial_{Z}\eta(Z)^{-1}dZ\Big)=\frac{-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\pi}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}(c_{r}+c_{r}^{2}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}).\end{split}

By Lemma
3.3
and (
A.1
), we know that for any
Y\geq 2Y_{c}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1,1}^{(2)}(Y)=\tilde{C}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}(\kappa\eta(Y))^{-1}(1+\mathcal{O}(|\kappa\eta(Y)|^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad\tilde{C}\in\mathbb{R},

which along with the fact
|\mathrm{Im}(\eta^{-1})(Y)|\leq c_{i}c_{r}^{-1}
for
Y\geq 2Y_{c}
, implies that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{2,1}|\leq C|\log|\varepsilon||,\quad|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{R}_{2,1})|\leq C(c_{i}c_{r}^{-1}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{6}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\ll C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{24}}.

The above bounds along with (
4.34
) deduce that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{2,1}|\leq C\text{ and }\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{I}_{2,1})=\frac{-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\pi}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{\frac{1}{3}}}+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{24}}+c_{r}^{2}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}).

By Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
3.4
and a similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
3.7
, we can infer that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{H}_{1,err1}(Z)u^{\prime\prime}\partial_{Y}\eta(Z)^{-1}\chi(Z)dZ\right|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Gathering the above results, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}\left(\mathcal{I}_{2}\right)\right|\leq C,\quad\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{I}_{2})=\frac{-u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})\pi}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{\frac{1}{3}}}+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{24}}+c_{r}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}).

The proof is completed. ∎

4.3.
The fast mode

This part is devoted to constructing the fast mode for the Orr-Sommerfeld equation. We construct the fast mode solution
\phi_{f}
to Orr-Sommerfeld equation around the solution
\psi_{a}
to the homogeneous Airy equation, as constructed in Theorem
3.10
. By our construction,
\psi_{a}(Y)
is a solution to the following Airy equation

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})w_{a}-(u-c)w_{a}=0,\quad(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{a}(Y)=w_{a}(Y),

\displaystyle\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{a}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}w_{a}(Y)=0.

Moreover, we notice that

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\psi_{a}-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\psi_{a}-u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{a}=-u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{a}.

Hence, to construct
\phi_{f}
, we need to construct a solution
\tilde{\phi}_{f}(Y)
to the equation:

(4.35)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{f}-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\tilde{\phi}_{f}-u^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{f}=u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{a}

with the following properties

\displaystyle\lim_{Y\to\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{f}(Y)=0,\quad|\tilde{\phi}_{f}(0)|\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\tilde{\phi}_{f}(0)|\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
with
c_{i}>-|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}/2
. Then there exists a solution
e^{\eta_{0}Y}\phi_{f}(Y)\in W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
to (
4.1
) such that

\displaystyle|\phi_{f}(0)-\psi_{a}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}(\phi_{f}-\psi_{a})(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Proof.

Firstly, according to Theorem
3.10
, we know that

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{a}-\psi_{a}^{(0)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\ll

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1}\sim|\psi_{a}^{(0)}(0)|\sim|\psi_{a}(0)|.

We apply Theorem
4.1
to construct the solution
\tilde{\phi}_{f}
to (
4.35
). To show that
\psi_{a}(0)
and
\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)
is the leading order of
\phi_{f}(0)
and
\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)
, respectively, We need to provide more precise estimates for
\tilde{\phi}_{f}
.

We define
\varphi_{f}^{(1)}(Y)=\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)+\varphi_{2}^{(1)}(Y)
satisfying
Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi_{f}^{(1)}]=u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{a}
, where

(4.36)

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y):=(u-\hat{c})\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ}{(u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c})^{2}}dY^{\prime},

and
\varphi_{2}^{(1)}(Y)
is the solution to the following system constructed in Proposition
2.1

(4.37)

\displaystyle Ray_{\hat{c}}[\varphi_{2}^{(0)}]=u^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{a}-\psi_{a}^{(0)})-\alpha^{2}(u-\hat{c})\varphi_{1}^{(1)}.

Now we show the estimates for
\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)
. By (
3.60
) and (
3.62
), we have that for any
Y\geq Y_{c}
,

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Y}^{+\infty}|u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c}|^{-1}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}|u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)|dZdY^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}|u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c}|^{-1}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Z}e^{-C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Z)-\eta(0)|}dZdY^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon||\log|\varepsilon||e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

For any
Y\in[0,Y_{c}]
, we write

(4.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)=&u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ-\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}dZ\\
&+\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime}))\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ}{(u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c})^{2}}dY^{\prime}.\end{split}

We find that for any
Y\in[0,Y_{c}]
,

(4.39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\int_{Y}^{+\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ\right|\leq&C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\kappa\eta(0)|^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Z)-\eta(0)||\kappa\eta(0)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}dZ\\
\leq&C|\varepsilon|.\end{split}

We notice that for any
Y\in[\delta|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}},Y_{c}]
with some
\delta>0
such that
|\eta(Y)-\eta(0)|\geq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}dZ\right|\leq

\displaystyle C|\hat{c}||\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}e^{-C|\kappa\eta(0)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Z}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\alpha||\log|\varepsilon||,

and for any
Y\in[0,\delta|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}dZ\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C|\hat{c}|\int_{Y}^{\delta|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}}\left|\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}\right|dZ+C|\hat{c}|\int_{\delta|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}}^{+\infty}\left|\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}\right|dZ

\displaystyle\quad\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\kappa\eta(0)|^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{Y}^{\delta|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}}e^{-C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta(Z)-\eta(0)||\kappa\eta(0)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}dZ+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\alpha||\log|\varepsilon||

\displaystyle\quad\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||.

By gathering the above two estimates, we obtain that for any
Y\in[0,Y_{c}]
,

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)}{u(Z)-\hat{c}}dZ\right|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||.

For the last term on the right hand side of (
4.38
), we have that for any
Y\in[0,Y_{c}]

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u(Y)-\hat{c}}{u^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{(u^{\prime}(Y_{c})-u^{\prime}(Y^{\prime}))\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ}{(u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c})^{2}}dY^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C|\hat{c}|\left|\int_{Y}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ\right|\int_{Y}^{+\infty}|u(Z)-\hat{c}|^{-1}dZ

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C|\varepsilon|\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||,

which along with (
4.38
), (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) gives that for any
Y\in[0,Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||.

Hence, we obtain

(4.41)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||.

By the definition of
\varphi_{1}^{(0)}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)=

\displaystyle u^{\prime}(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ}{(u(Y^{\prime})-\hat{c})^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{\int_{Y}^{\infty}u^{\prime\prime}(Z)\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Z)dZ}{u(Y)-\hat{c}}.

Hence, by a similar argument as above, we can obtain

(4.42)

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\alpha\|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||.

Moreover, by the definition of
\varphi_{1,1}^{(0)}
, we have

\displaystyle(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)=u^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(Y)+\psi_{a}^{(0)}(Y)).

Then we get

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})\partial^{2}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+C\|\psi_{a}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\kappa\eta(0)|^{-1}\leq C|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial^{3}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\|(u-\hat{c})^{3}\partial^{4}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}.

Hence, we obtain

(4.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\varphi_{1}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||,\\
&\|(u-\hat{c})\partial^{2}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1},\quad\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial^{3}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
&\|(u-\hat{c})^{3}\partial^{4}_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C\alpha^{2}.\end{split}

In particular, we have

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}+\alpha)|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}^{(1)}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Now we turn to show the estimates for
\varphi_{2}^{(1)}(Y)
. By Proposition
2.1
, Theorem
3.10
and (
4.43
), we have

\displaystyle\|(u-\hat{c})\partial^{2}_{Y}\varphi_{2}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{2}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\varphi_{2}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\log|\hat{c}_{i}||\|u^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{a}-\psi_{a}^{(0)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+C\alpha^{2}\|\varphi^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1},

and

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{2}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq\|\varphi_{2}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq

\displaystyle C|\log|\varepsilon||\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|(u-\hat{c})^{j}\partial_{Y}^{j}(u^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{a}-\psi_{a}^{(0)})-\alpha^{2}(u-\hat{c})\varphi_{1}^{(1)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||.

The above estimates along with (
4.43
) deduce

(4.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha^{2}\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\varphi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\varphi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||,\\
&\|\varphi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq C\alpha^{2}.\end{split}

In particular, we obtain

(4.45)

\displaystyle|\varphi^{(1)}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\varphi^{(1)}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle OS[\psi_{a}+\varphi_{f}^{(1)}]=\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\varphi_{f}^{(1)}.

Next we define
\psi^{(1)}_{f}=\psi^{(1)}_{f,1}+\psi^{(1)}_{f,2}+\psi^{(1)}_{f,3}
,
where
\psi^{(1)}_{f,1},\psi^{(1)}_{f,2}
and
\psi^{(1)}_{f,3}
are the solutions to (
4.8
) by taking
\varphi^{(j)}=\varphi_{f}^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(j-1)}=\psi_{a}
. Then by (
4.9
), we have

\displaystyle\|\psi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq C(\alpha+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\hat{c}_{i}|^{-1})|\log|\varepsilon||^{2}(\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}).

Moreover, by Theorem
3.9
and Theorem
3.8
, we can obtain

(4.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi^{(1)}_{f,1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi^{(1)}_{f,1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi^{(f)}_{f,1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\\
&\leq C|\varepsilon||\log|\varepsilon||\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{Y}^{j}\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}([1/2,+\infty))}+\|\partial_{Y}^{j}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}([1/2,+\infty))}\Big),\\
&\leq C|\varepsilon||\log|\varepsilon||\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|(u-\hat{c})^{j}\partial_{Y}^{j}\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})^{j}\partial_{Y}^{j}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big),\end{split}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi^{(1)}_{f,2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi^{(1)}_{f,2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi^{(f)}_{f,2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\\
&\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\log|\varepsilon||(\|\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}),\end{split}

and

(4.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi^{(1)}_{f,3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi^{(1)}_{f,3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi^{(f)}_{f,3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\\
&\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||(\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
&\quad+C\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||(\|\varphi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}).\end{split}

We also notice that by Theorem
3.10
, (
3.60
) and (
3.62
),

(4.49)

\displaystyle\alpha^{-2}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{6}}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}.

Therefore, by (
4.44
), (
4.46
)-(
4.49
), we obtain

(4.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\psi_{f}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq C\alpha^{3}|\log|\varepsilon||^{2},\\
&\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi^{(f)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||^{2}\ll|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1}.\end{split}

In particular, we have

(4.51)

\displaystyle|\psi_{f}^{(1)}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||\ll\psi_{a}(0)\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\psi_{f}^{(1)}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Moreover,
OS[\psi_{a}+\varphi_{f}^{(1)}+\psi_{f}^{(1)}]=u^{\prime\prime}\psi_{f}^{(1)}
. We define that
\tilde{\phi}_{f}^{(1)}
is the solution to
OS[\tilde{\phi}_{f}^{(1)}]=-u^{\prime\prime}\psi^{(1)}_{f}
constructed in Theorem
4.1
. Then by Theorem
4.1
, we find that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\phi}_{f}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq

\displaystyle C|\log|\varepsilon||(\|(u-\hat{c})^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}\psi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|(u-\hat{c})\partial_{Y}\psi^{(1)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\psi^{(f)}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||^{3}\ll|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-1}.

In particular, we have

(4.52)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\phi}^{(1)}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\tilde{\phi}^{(1)}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

Moreover,
OS[\psi_{a}+\varphi_{f}^{(1)}+\psi_{f}^{(1)}+\tilde{\phi}_{f}^{(1)}]=0
.
Then we take
\tilde{\phi}_{f}=\varphi_{f}^{(1)}+\psi_{f}^{(1)}+\tilde{\phi}_{f}^{(1)}
, and by (
4.45
),(
4.51
) and (
4.52
), we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{\phi}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\psi_{a}(0)|\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}\tilde{\phi}_{f}(0)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\log|\varepsilon||\ll|\partial_{Y}\psi_{a}(0)|.

The proof is completed.
∎

5.
Dispersion relation and T-S waves

In this section, we construct a non-trivial solution
\phi(Y)
to the homogeneous Orr-Sommerfeld equation with non-slip boundary condition

(5.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}\phi-(u-c)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi+u^{\prime\prime}\phi=0,\\
&\phi(0)=\partial_{Y}\phi(0)=0,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\phi(Y)=0.\end{split}

We shall construct such a solution
\phi
by a linear combination of the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and the fast mode
\phi_{f}
, which are constructed in Theorem
4.2
and Theorem
4.5
, respectively. That is, for certain
\alpha\ll 1
, there exists a
c\in\mathbb{C}
such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\phi=C_{s}(\alpha)\phi_{s}+C_{f}(\alpha)\phi_{f}

with
C_{s}
and
C_{f}
are not zero. Since
\phi_{s}
and
\phi_{f}
are both solutions to the homogeneous Orr-Sommerfeld equation, we only need to match the non-slip boundary condition by choosing appropriate
C_{s}
and
C_{f}
. In details,

\displaystyle C_{s}\phi_{s}(0)+C_{f}\phi_{f}(0)=0\text{ and }C_{s}\partial_{Y}\phi_{s}(0)+C_{f}\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)=0.

Hence, the existence of the non-zero constants
C_{s}
and
C_{f}
is guaranteed by the following dispersion relation:

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{s}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{s}(0)}=\frac{\phi_{f}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\phi_{f}(0)}.

The task of this section is to find
c\in\mathbb{C}
such that (
5.3
) holds for certain small
\alpha
. To ensure the existences of the slow mode
\phi_{s}
and the fast mode
\phi_{f}
, we assume
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
.
By Theorem
4.2
and Theorem
4.5
, we know that (
5.3
) is equivalent to

(5.4)

\displaystyle c-u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha+\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}+\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)=0,

where
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)
is smooth on
c
and analytic on
\alpha
. We point out that the solutions to the Rayleigh equation are analytic on
c
and
\alpha
, and the solutions to the Airy equation are smooth on
c_{r},c_{i}
and analytic on
\alpha
for
\alpha\neq 0
(here we recall the relation
\varepsilon=-\mathrm{i}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-1}
). Moreover, by the definitions of
A_{j}(Y)
with
j=1,2
, we know that
|\partial_{c_{r}}A_{j}(k,Y)+|\partial_{c_{i}}A_{j}(k,Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|A_{j}(k-1,Y)|
and
|\partial_{c_{r}}\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{j}(Y)|+|\partial_{c_{r}}\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{j}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\partial_{Y}^{k+1}A_{j}(Y)|
.
Hence, we have that for any
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)|\leq C(\alpha^{2}+|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}})|\log|\varepsilon||,\quad|\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)|\leq C(\nu),\\
&|\partial_{c_{r}}\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)|+|\partial_{c_{i}}\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\log|\varepsilon||.\end{split}

Moreover, if
\alpha\gg|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{4}}
and
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
, then we have the following formula for the imaginary part of (
5.4
):

(5.6)

\displaystyle c_{i}-\frac{\alpha^{2}u^{\prime\prime}(0)\pi}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}+\mathrm{Im}\Big(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\Big)+o(\alpha^{2})=0.

Here we used the fact
u^{\prime}(Y_{c})=u^{\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}(\alpha)
adn
u^{\prime\prime}(Y_{c})=u^{\prime\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}(\alpha)
.

We first provide a priori estimate for
c(\alpha)
solving (
5.4
).

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\nu\ll 1
. Then there exist
A\leq 1\ll B
independent of
\nu
such that for any
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
, the solution
c(\alpha)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
to (
5.3
)
continuously depends on
\alpha
satisfying the following properties:

•

There exists
A_{1}\geq A_{c}\geq A_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))<0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))>0.

•

There exits
B_{c}\leq B_{0}\leq B_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))>0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))<0.

•

If
\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\ll\alpha\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
, then

\displaystyle\alpha\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Proof.

From (
5.4
), we know that

(5.7)

\displaystyle c=u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha-\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}-\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c).

Case 1.
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
.
Let
\alpha=A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
. Then we first have that
|\varepsilon|=A^{-1}\nu^{\frac{3}{8}}
. For the case
A\gg 1
such that
|\kappa\eta(0)|>M
, we get by Lemma
A.5
that

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}=

\displaystyle-e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+o(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}),

which implies that

\displaystyle c_{r}=u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}+C_{0}A^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\frac{3}{16}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+o(\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}).

Then we can obtain that
c_{r}\sim A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
. Moreover, we have

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c)=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)+o(\nu^{\frac{1}{8}})\geq CA^{-1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}=C^{\prime}A^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}.

On the other hand, for the case of
A\sim 1
, we rewrite (
5.7
) as

\displaystyle 1=\frac{\alpha}{u^{\prime}(0)c}+\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{-c\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}),

which along with Lemma
A.5
implies that

\displaystyle 1=\frac{\alpha}{u^{\prime}(0)c}+\frac{\mathcal{A}(2,0)}{\kappa\eta(0)\mathcal{A}(1,0)}+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}).

Therefore, we obtain

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(-\kappa\eta(0))+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\frac{1}{16}})=\frac{u^{\prime}(0)c}{\alpha},

where
\mathcal{F}(z)
is the function defined in
[
26
]
related to the Hankel function or the Tietjens function. By the property of
\mathcal{F}(z)
(see Fig 3.2 in
[
26
]
), we know that there exists
z_{0}\in[2,2.5]
such that
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}(z_{0}))=0
and
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}(z))<0
for any
z\in[2,z_{0})
. Then there exits
z_{1}=a_{1}+b_{1}i
with
a_{1}<z_{0}
and
|b_{1}|\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{F}(z_{0}+z_{1}))\in[2,3]
,
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}(z_{0}+z_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}))<0
and
|\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}(z_{0}+z_{1})+O(\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}))|>\frac{1}{8}\nu^{\frac{1}{16}}
. Moreover, we have
|z_{0}+z_{1}|\in[2,2.5]
. Then there exists a small
r_{0}
such that for any
-\kappa\eta(0)\in B(z_{0}+z_{1},r_{0})
, we always have
c_{i}<0
.

In particular, we take
\mathrm{Re}(-\kappa\eta(0))=\mathrm{Re}(z_{0}+z_{1})
, which implies

\displaystyle c_{r}=u^{\prime}(0)^{\frac{2}{3}}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathrm{Re}(z_{0}+z_{1}).

By (
5.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle A_{0}=u^{\prime}(0)^{\frac{5}{3}}\frac{\mathrm{Re}(z_{0}+z_{1})}{\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{F}(z_{0}+z_{1}))},

where
\alpha=A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
.
As a consequence, we infer that there exists
A_{0}\sim 1
such that
c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}})\in\mathbb{H}
with
\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))<0
.

Since
c
depends on
\alpha
continuously, we obtain that there exist
A_{0},A_{c},A_{1}
such that
\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))<0
,
\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))=0
and
\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))>0
.

Case 2.
\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\ll\alpha\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
.
We denote
\alpha=A\nu^{\gamma}
with
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{8},\frac{1}{12})
. In this case, we first notice that by Lemma
A.5
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))=

\displaystyle CA^{-1}\nu^{\frac{1}{4}-\gamma}-A^{-3}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2}|\log|\varepsilon||)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle CA^{-1}\nu^{\frac{1}{4}-\gamma}(1+o(1))>0,

provide that
\nu^{\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma}\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{4}-\beta}
for any
\gamma>\frac{1}{8}
and
\alpha^{2}|\log|\varepsilon||=A^{2}\nu^{2\gamma}|\log\nu|\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{4}-\gamma}
for any
\gamma<\frac{1}{12}
.

Case 3.
\alpha\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
.

Let
\alpha=B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
. In this case, by (
5.6
), we know that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))=\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}\alpha^{2}\pi-\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)+o(\alpha^{2}).

We first notice that for
B\ll 1
,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))=&\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}B^{2}\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}+C_{Ai}B^{-\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}+o(\nu^{\frac{1}{6}})\\
\geq&C(B^{-\frac{3}{2}}-B^{2})\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}>0.\end{split}

Here
C_{Ai}
is a positive constant just relying on Airy functions.
On the other hand, for
B\gg 1

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{\prime\prime}(0)}{u^{\prime}(0)^{2}}B^{2}\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}+C_{Ai}B^{-\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}+o(\nu^{\frac{1}{6}})

\displaystyle\leq-C(B^{2}-B^{-\frac{3}{2}})\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}<0.

Therefore, there exists
B_{c}>1
such that for
\alpha=B_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))=0.

The proof is completed.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\nu\ll 1
. Then for any
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
with
A\leq 1\ll B
, there exists a unique
c(\alpha)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
solving (
5.4
). Moreover,
c(\alpha)
depends on
\alpha
continuously .

Proof.

We introduce

\mathbb{F}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\big(F_{r}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i}),F_{i}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})\big),

where

\displaystyle F_{r}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\mathrm{Re}\left(c-u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha+\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}+\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)\right),

\displaystyle F_{i}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\mathrm{Im}\left(c-u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha+\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}+\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c)\right).

We need to show the Jacobian determinant of
F_{r}
and
F_{i}
is non-zero for
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
. For this purpose, by Lemma
A.5
, we have that for
j=1,2
,

\displaystyle\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}=\kappa^{-1}u^{\prime}(0)\frac{\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))}+\mathcal{O}(|c|^{2}).

By our definition of
\eta(Y)
, we know that

\displaystyle\eta(0;c_{r},c_{i})=-u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}(c_{r}+\mathrm{i}c_{i})+\mathcal{O}(|c|^{2}),

and smoothly depends on
c_{r}
and
c_{i}
. Therefore, we obtain

(5.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\partial_{c_{r}}\left(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)=-1+\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(|c|),\\
&\partial_{c_{i}}\left(\frac{u(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)=-\mathrm{i}+\mathrm{i}\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(|c|).\end{split}

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{c_{r}}F_{r}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(|c|),

\displaystyle\partial_{c_{r}}F_{i}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(|c|),

\displaystyle\partial_{c_{r}}F_{i}(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=-\mathrm{Im}\left(\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(|c|),

\displaystyle\partial_{c_{i}}F(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(|c|).

Hence, the Jacobian determinant
J(F_{r},F_{i})(\alpha;c_{r},c_{i})
of
F_{r}
and
F_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle J(F_{r},F_{i})(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right|^{2}+\mathcal{O}(|c|).

And by Lemma
A.5
, we know that for any
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right|\sim 1.

Therefore, for
\nu\ll 1
, we obtain that for any
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
,

(5.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}J(F_{r},F_{i})(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{i};c_{r},c_{i})=&\left|\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))^{2}}\right|^{2}+\mathcal{O}(|c|)\\
\geq&C(1-|c|)\geq\frac{1}{2}C>0.\end{split}

On the other hand, since
F_{r},F_{i}
are both analytic on
\alpha
, for any fixed
\nu
, we have

(5.13)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\alpha}F_{r}(\alpha;c_{r},c_{i})|+|\partial_{\alpha}F_{i}(\alpha;c_{r},c_{i})|\leq C..

We denote

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}(c^{(j)})=\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\text{ with taking }c=c^{(j)}.

We now show that there exists a
(\alpha^{0},c^{0})\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
such that
\mathbb{F}(\alpha^{0};c_{r}^{0},c_{i}^{0})=0
. Let
\alpha=A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
with
A\gg 1
. We take
c^{(0)}=u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha
, and for
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle c^{(j)}=u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha-\mathbb{A}(c^{(j-1)})-\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c^{(j-1)}).

By Lemma
A.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}(c^{(0)})=-A^{-1}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}+\mathcal{O}(A^{-\frac{4}{3}})\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},

which implies

\displaystyle c^{(1)}=\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\left(u^{\prime}(0)A+A^{-1}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(A^{-\frac{4}{3}})\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c^{(1)})=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}A^{-1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}+\mathcal{O}(A^{-\frac{4}{3}})\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}.

Hence,
(\alpha,c^{(1)})\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
.
By the inductive argument and Lemma
A.5
, we can obtain that for any
j\geq 1
,
(\alpha,c^{(j)})\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
. Moreover, again by Lemma
A.5
and (
5.11
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{c_{r}}\left(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)\right|+\left|\partial_{c_{i}}\left(\frac{u^{\prime}(0)\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}\right)\right|\leq CA^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

Therefore, we obtain that for any
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle|c^{(j+1)}-c^{(j)}|\leq

\displaystyle CA^{-\frac{4}{3}}|c^{(j)}-c^{(j-1)}|+C|\varepsilon\log|\varepsilon|||c^{(j)}-c^{(j-1)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CA^{-\frac{4}{3}}|c^{(j)}-c^{(j-1)}|.

Since
A\gg 1
, there exists
c^{0}
such that
\lim_{j\to\infty}c^{(j)}=c^{0}
and
c^{0}
satisfies

\displaystyle c^{0}=u^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\alpha-\mathbb{A}(c^{0})-\mathcal{R}_{d}(\alpha,c^{(0)}).

Hence,
(\alpha,c_{r}^{(0)},c_{i}^{(0)})
is a zero point of
\mathbb{F}(\alpha;c_{r},c_{i})
, which along with (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) implies that for
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
, there exists a unique
c(\alpha)
solving (
5.4
) with
(c(\alpha),\alpha)\in\mathbb{H}_{3}
. Moreover,
c(\alpha)
depends on
\alpha
continuously.
∎

By Proposition
5.1
and
5.2
, we conclude the following result.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\nu\ll 1
. Then there exist
A\leq 1\ll B
independent of
\nu
such that for any
\alpha\in(A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}},B\nu^{\frac{1}{12}})
, there exists a pair
(c,\phi)
solving (
5.1
) with
\phi\in W^{4,\infty}
. Moreover,
c
satisfies the following properties

•

There exists
A_{1}\geq A_{c}\geq A_{0}>A
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))<0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(A_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}))>0.

•

There exists
B_{c}\leq B_{0}\leq B_{1}<B
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))>0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{c}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))=0,\quad\mathrm{Im}(c(B_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}))<0.

•

If
\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\ll\alpha\ll\nu^{\frac{1}{12}}
, then

\displaystyle\alpha\mathrm{Im}(c(\alpha))\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Appendix A
The Airy function

Let
Ai(y)
be the Airy function, which is a nontrivial solution of
f^{\prime\prime}-yf=0
. By
[
29
]
, we have the following asymptotic formula for
|\arg z|\leq\pi-\delta
with
\delta>0
and
|z|\geq M
for some large
M
,

(A.1)

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{z}Ai(z)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}z^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{k}{2}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}z^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+R(z)),\quad R(z)=\mathcal{O}(z^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad\forall k=0,1,2.

We denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(1,z):=-\int_{z}^{+\infty}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}t)dt,\quad\mathcal{A}(2,z):=-\int_{z}^{+\infty}\mathcal{A}(1,t)dt,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(1,z):=\int_{-\infty}^{z}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}t)dt,\quad\mathcal{C}(2,z):=\int_{-\infty}^{z}\mathcal{B}(1,t)dt.

Lemma A.1

(Lemma A.2 in
[
27
]
)
.

Suppose that
\delta_{0}
is a small positive constant. Assume that
\mathrm{Im}z\leq\delta_{0}
and
|z|\geq M
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(1,z)=-e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}z)^{-\frac{3}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}z)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{R}_{1}(z)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(2,z)=e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}z)^{-\frac{5}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}z)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{R}_{2}(z)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(1,z)=-e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}z)^{-\frac{3}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}z)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{R}_{3}(z)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(2,z)=e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}z)^{-\frac{5}{4}}e^{-\frac{2}{3}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}z)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\mathcal{R}_{4}(z)),

where
\mathcal{R}_{i}(z)=\mathcal{O}(z^{-\frac{3}{2}})
for
i=1,2,3,4.

Lemma A.2

(Lemma A.3 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
\delta_{0}>0
be a small constant. Suppose
-\delta_{0}<\mathrm{Im}Z_{0}<\delta_{0}
,
\mathrm{Re}Z_{0}<0
. There exists
M_{1}>0
such that if
|\mathrm{Re}(Y+Z_{0})|>M_{1}\delta_{0}
, then for any
0\leq Z\leq Y
,

\displaystyle\left|e^{-\frac{2}{3}(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}(Y+Z_{0}))^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{-(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}(Z+Z_{0}))^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right|\leq e^{-C_{1}|Y-Z|(|Y+Z_{0}|+|Z+Z_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Next, we present the asymptotic formula for the classical Scorer function, which can be found in
[
29
]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Assume
|\arg z|\leq\frac{2\pi}{3}-\delta
, where
\delta
is an arbitrarily small positive constant. Then the following asymptotic expansion holds:

(A.2)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{z}Hi(-t)dt\sim\pi^{-1}\log z+\frac{2\gamma+\log 3}{3\pi}+\pi^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k-1}\frac{(3k-1)!}{k!(3z^{3})^{k}},\quad z\to\infty.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\delta_{0}>0
be a small constant as in Lemma
A.1
and
\hat{c}:=c+\mathrm{i}|\varepsilon|\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}
. Suppose
-\delta_{0}<\mathrm{Im}(\kappa\eta(0))<\delta_{0}
and
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{2}
. Then for any
Y\in[0,2Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)A_{1}(Y)-A_{1}(1,Y)A_{2}(Y)=\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}Hi(e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}).

Proof.

By the definition of
A_{j}(Y)
and
A_{j}(1,Y)
, we know that

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)A_{1}(Y)-A_{1}(1,Y)A_{2}(Y)=

\displaystyle-2\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\mathrm{i}\int_{-\infty}^{Y}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z))dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))

\displaystyle-2\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\mathrm{i}\int^{+\infty}_{Y}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z))dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)).

We also notice that

\displaystyle\int^{+\infty}_{Y}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z))dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))=

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\partial_{Z}(\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Z))\partial_{Z}\eta^{-1}dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa^{-1}\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{R}_{1}(Y),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))(1-\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)^{-1})

\displaystyle+\kappa^{-1}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Z))\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\eta}{(\partial_{Z}\eta)^{2}}dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)).

On the other hand, by Lemma
3.1
, we know that for any
Y\in[0,2Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle|1-\partial_{Y}\eta(Y)^{-1}|\leq Cc_{r}\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta(Y)/(\partial_{Y}\eta(Y))^{2}|\leq C,

which along with Lemma
A.1
and
A.2
implies that for
Y\in[0,2Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{1}(Y)|\lesssim\kappa^{-1}(c_{r}+\kappa^{-1})\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}.

Hence, we obtain that for any
Y\in[0,2Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle\int^{+\infty}_{Y}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z))dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))=-\kappa^{-1}\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}).

Similarly, we can obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{Y}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z))dZAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))=\kappa^{-1}\mathcal{C}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}).

Thus, we can write that for any
Y\in[0,2Y_{c}]
,

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)A_{1}(Y)-A_{1}(1,Y)A_{2}(Y)

\displaystyle=-2\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\mathrm{i}[\mathcal{C}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))-\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(Y))Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))]+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r})

\displaystyle=2\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\int_{-\infty}^{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}z)dzAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\int_{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Z)}^{+\infty}Ai(z)dzAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}),

which along with the facts
Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}z)=\frac{1}{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}(Ai(z)-\mathrm{i}Bi(z))
,
\int_{-\infty}^{0}Bi(z)dz=0
and
Gi(z)+\mathrm{i}Ai(z)=e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}Hi(e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}z)
, implies that

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)A_{1}(Y)-A_{1}(1,Y)A_{2}(Y)

\displaystyle=\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{3}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}Ai(z)dzAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))

\displaystyle\quad-\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)}Bi(z)dzAi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))

\displaystyle\quad+\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{6}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\int_{e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y)}^{+\infty}Ai(z)dzBi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r})

\displaystyle=\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{5\pi}{6}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\mathrm{i}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))+Gi(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(Y))\Big)+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r})

\displaystyle=\pi|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}u^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{2}{3}}Hi(e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\kappa\eta(Y))+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{r}).

The proof is completed.
∎

Lemma A.5

(Lemma A.5 in
[
27
]
)
.

Let
|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1\ll\kappa
and
c_{r}>0
. Suppose
|\kappa\eta_{i}|<\delta_{0}
. Then there holds

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)-\kappa^{-1}\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))\right|+\kappa\left|\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)-\kappa^{-2}\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))\right|\leq C\kappa^{-1}(|c|+\kappa^{-1}),

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}-\kappa^{-1}\frac{\mathcal{A}(2,\kappa\eta(0))}{\mathcal{A}(1,\kappa\eta(0))}\right|\leq C\kappa^{-1}(\kappa^{-1}+|c|).

Moreover, if
|\kappa\eta(0)|>M
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)}=

\displaystyle-e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{4}-\frac{\theta_{0}}{2})}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+o(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}),\quad\frac{Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}}\kappa\eta(0))\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(2,0)}{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(1,0)^{2}}=1+\mathcal{O}(|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{r}^{-\frac{3}{2}}).
